\title{The toral subgroup of $G(\mathfrak{m})$ normalizes the unipotent subgroup: a machine-checked proof}
\date{}
\begin{document}
\maketitle
\begin{abstract}
We present a formal verification of Theorem 3.4 of the source. The theorem concerns the group $G(\mathfrak{m})$, defined by generators and relations and associated with the Monster Lie algebra $\mathfrak{m}$. It states that the toral subgroup $H$, generated by the elements $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$, normalizes the unipotent subgroup $U$, generated by all unipotent generators. The formalization replaces the coefficients $c(j)$ of the modular function $J$ by an arbitrary function $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$, and the theorem is proved with no hypothesis on $c$. The verified proof follows the source argument: conjugation by a generator of $H$ sends a unipotent generator to another unipotent generator, and this extends to the generated subgroups. The one point the source leaves implicit is checked explicitly, namely that the inverses of the generators of $H$ are again generators of $H$.
\end{abstract}

\section{Statement}

Let $\mathbb{C}$ denote the complex numbers and $\mathbb{N}$ the natural numbers. Let $c(j)$ be the coefficient of $q^j$ in the modular function
\[
J(q)=\sum_{j\geq -1}c(j)q^j=\frac{1}{q}+196884\,q+21493760\,q^2+864299970\,q^3+\cdots,
\]
so that $c(-1)=1$, $c(0)=0$, $c(1)=196884,\dots$.

\paragraph{Index set and constants.} Define the index set
\[
E=\{(\ell,j,k)\mid j\in\mathbb{N},\ 1\leq k\leq c(j),\ 0\leq \ell<j\}.
\]
For indices $\ell,j$ put
\[
c_{\ell j}:=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell)\in\mathbb{C}.
\]

\paragraph{Generators.} Let $\mathcal{X}$ be the set of symbols
\[
\mathcal{X}=\{H_1(s),\,H_2(s),\,X_{-1}(u),\,Y_{-1}(u),\,X_{\ell,jk}(u),\,Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\mid s\in\Cx,\ u\in\mathbb{C},\ (\ell,j,k)\in E\},
\]
and let $F(\mathcal{X})$ be the free group on $\mathcal{X}$. The symbols $X_{-1}(u)$, $Y_{-1}(u)$, $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$, $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ are called \emph{unipotent generators}. The commutator is $(a,b):=aba^{-1}b^{-1}$.

\paragraph{Relations.} For all $s,t\in\Cx$, $u,v\in\mathbb{C}$ and $(\ell,j,k),(m,p,q)\in E$, the set $\mathcal{R}\subseteq F(\mathcal{X})$ and the auxiliary elements $\tw$ are given as follows.
\begin{align*}
\tag{Re:0} \tw_{-1}(s)&:=X_{-1}(s)\,Y_{-1}(-s^{-1})\,X_{-1}(s),\qquad \tw_{-1}:=\tw_{-1}(1),\\
\tag{Im:0} \tw_{\ell,jk}(s)&:=X_{\ell,jk}(s)\,Y_{\ell,jk}\Big(\frac{-s^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Big)\,X_{\ell,jk}(s),\qquad \tw_{\ell,jk}:=\tw_{\ell,jk}(1),\\
\intertext{relations defining $\mathrm{GL}_2(-1)$:}
\tag{H:1a} H_1(s)H_1(t)&=H_1(st),\\
\tag{H:1b} H_2(s)H_2(t)&=H_2(st),\\
\tag{H:2} H_1(s)H_2(t)&=H_2(t)H_1(s),\\
\tag{Re:1a} X_{-1}(u)X_{-1}(v)&=X_{-1}(u+v),\\
\tag{Re:1b} Y_{-1}(u)Y_{-1}(v)&=Y_{-1}(u+v),\\
\tag{Re:2} Y_{-1}(-t)X_{-1}(s)Y_{-1}(t)&=X_{-1}(-t^{-1})\,Y_{-1}(-t^{2}s)\,X_{-1}(t^{-1}),\\
\tag{Re:3} \tw_{-1}(s)\tw_{-1}&=H_1(-s)H_2(-s^{-1}),\\
\tag{Re:4a} \tw_{-1}X_{-1}(u)\tw_{-1}^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(-u),\\
\tag{Re:4b} \tw_{-1}Y_{-1}(u)\tw_{-1}^{-1}&=X_{-1}(-u),\\
\tag{Re:5a} \tw_{-1}H_1(s)\tw_{-1}^{-1}&=H_2(s),\\
\tag{Re:5b} \tw_{-1}H_2(s)\tw_{-1}^{-1}&=H_1(s),\\
\tag{Re:6a} H_1(s)X_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=X_{-1}(su),\\
\tag{Re:6b} H_2(s)X_{-1}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=X_{-1}(s^{-1}u),\\
\tag{Re:6c} H_1(s)Y_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(s^{-1}u),\\
\tag{Re:6d} H_2(s)Y_{-1}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(su),\\
\intertext{relations defining $\mathrm{GL}_2(\ell,j,k)$:}
\tag{Im:1a} X_{\ell,jk}(u)X_{\ell,jk}(v)&=X_{\ell,jk}(u+v),\\
\tag{Im:1b} Y_{\ell,jk}(u)Y_{\ell,jk}(v)&=Y_{\ell,jk}(u+v),\\
\tag{Im:2} Y_{\ell,jk}(-t)X_{\ell,jk}(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(t)&=X_{\ell,jk}\Big(\frac{-t^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Big)\,Y_{\ell,jk}(-c_{\ell j}t^2s)\,X_{\ell,jk}\Big(\frac{t^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\Big),\\
\tag{Im:3} \tw_{\ell,jk}\big(s^{(\ell+1)(j-\ell)}\big)\tw_{\ell,jk}&=H_1\big((-s)^{j-\ell}\big)H_2\big((-s)^{\ell+1}\big),\\
\tag{Im:4a} \tw_{\ell,jk}X_{\ell,jk}(u)\tw_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}\Big(\frac{-u}{c_{\ell j}}\Big),\\
\tag{Im:4b} \tw_{\ell,jk}Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\tw_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(-c_{\ell j}u),\\
\tag{Im:5a} \tw_{\ell,jk}H_1(s^{j-\ell})\tw_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=H_2(s^{-(\ell+1)}),\\
\tag{Im:5b} \tw_{\ell,jk}H_2(s^{\ell+1})\tw_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=H_1(s^{-(j-\ell)}),\\
\tag{Im:6a} H_1(s)X_{\ell,jk}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(s^{\ell+1}u),\\
\tag{Im:6b} H_2(s)X_{\ell,jk}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(s^{j-\ell}u),\\
\tag{Im:6c} H_1(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(s^{-(\ell+1)}u),\\
\tag{Im:6d} H_2(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(s^{-(j-\ell)}u),\\
\intertext{relations between unipotent generators:}
\tag{U:1a} (X_{-1}(u),X_{j-1,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1b} (Y_{-1}(u),Y_{j-1,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1c} (Y_{-1}(u),X_{0,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1d} (X_{-1}(u),Y_{0,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:2} (X_{\ell,jk}(u),Y_{m,pq}(v))&=1\quad\text{for } j\neq p,\text{ or }k\neq q,\text{ or }|\ell-m|>1,\\
\tag{U:3a} (X_{1,2k}(u),Y_{0,2k}(v))&=X_{-1}(uv),\\
\tag{U:3b} (X_{0,2k}(u),Y_{1,2k}(v))&=Y_{-1}(uv),\\
\intertext{action of $\tw_{-1}$ on unipotent generators:}
\tag{U:4a} \tw_{-1}X_{\ell,jk}(u)\tw_{-1}^{-1}&=X_{j-1-\ell,jk}\big((-1)^{j-\ell-1}u\big),\\
\tag{U:4b} \tw_{-1}Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\tw_{-1}^{-1}&=Y_{j-1-\ell,jk}\big((-1)^{j-\ell-1}u\big).
\end{align*}

\paragraph{The group and its subgroups.} The group is $G(\mathfrak{m})=\langle\mathcal{X}\mid\mathcal{R}\rangle=F(\mathcal{X})/N_{\mathcal{R}}$, where $N_{\mathcal{R}}$ is the normal closure of $\mathcal{R}$.
\begin{itemize}
\item The \emph{toral subgroup} $H\le G(\mathfrak{m})$ is generated by $\{H_1(s),H_2(s)\mid s\in\Cx\}$.
\item The \emph{unipotent subgroup} $U\le G(\mathfrak{m})$ is generated by all unipotent generators:
\[
U=\langle X_{-1}(u),\,Y_{-1}(u),\,X_{\ell,jk}(u),\,Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\mid u\in\mathbb{C},\ (\ell,j,k)\in E\rangle.
\]
\end{itemize}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem 3.4 of the source]
The toral subgroup $H$ normalizes the unipotent subgroup $U$. That is, for every $h\in H$ and every $u\in U$, the conjugate $huh^{-1}$ lies in $U$.
\end{theorem}

\section{Formalization}

\subsection{The library}

The presentation of $G(\mathfrak{m})$ comes from an earlier verified development (the library). The same library also contains the proof that $G(\mathfrak{m})$ is perfect (Theorem 3.3, \leanname{gsimple_g_m_perfect}) together with its auxiliary lemmas. Those results are not used here, apart from the two theorems named below.

\paragraph{Index set and constants.}
\begin{itemize}
\item The coefficients of $J$ are replaced by a parameter $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$.
\item The predicate $\leanname{Valid}\,c\,\ell\,j\,k$ means $\ell<j$ and $1\le k\le c(j)$, with $\ell,j,k\in\mathbb{N}$. This is exactly $(\ell,j,k)\in E$, with $c$ in place of the coefficients of $J$.
\end{itemize}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{Valid} --- library, verified earlier}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Index set E: (ℓ,j,k) with 0 ≤ ℓ \PYZlt{} j and 1 ≤ k ≤ c(j). }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Valid}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZlt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

\paragraph{Generators.} The type \leanname{Gen} $c$ is the set $\mathcal{X}$ of generating symbols:
\begin{itemize}
\item $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$ with $s\in\Cx$, a unit of $\mathbb{C}$;
\item $X_{-1}(u)$ and $Y_{-1}(u)$ with $u\in\mathbb{C}$;
\item $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ and $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ with $u\in\mathbb{C}$ and a proof of $\leanname{Valid}\,c\,\ell\,j\,k$.
\end{itemize}
The membership proof is a proposition, so it carries no information. Its only role is to ensure that $X_{\ell,jk}$ and $Y_{\ell,jk}$ are indexed exactly by $E$.

\paragraph{The constant $c_{\ell j}$.} \leanname{cc} is the complex number $(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell)$, where $j-1$ and $j-\ell$ are computed in $\mathbb{N}$. For $(\ell,j,k)\in E$ we have $\ell\le j-1$, so all three factors are nonzero. Hence $c_{\ell j}\neq 0$, and the divisions by $c_{\ell j}$ in the relations mean what they mean in the source.
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{cc} --- library, verified earlier}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]

\PY{k+kn}{noncomputable}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{cc}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZca{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{choose}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}

\paragraph{Commutators and relators.}
\begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{cm} is the commutator $(a,b)=aba^{-1}b^{-1}$.
\item \leanname{eqr}$(a,b)=ab^{-1}$ is the relator that encodes the equation $a=b$.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Relations.} The inductive predicate \leanname{Rel} $c$ has one constructor for each tagged family of relations (H:1a)--(U:4b). Each constructor has the parameters of the source:
\begin{itemize}
\item $s,t\in\Cx$ and $u,v\in\mathbb{C}$;
\item membership proofs in $E$ for every index triple that occurs, namely $(j-1,j,k)$ in (U:1a),(U:1b); $(0,j,k)$ in (U:1c),(U:1d); $(1,2,k)$ and $(0,2,k)$ in (U:3a),(U:3b); and both $(\ell,j,k)$ and $(j-1-\ell,j,k)$ in (U:4a),(U:4b), the latter being automatic;
\item for (U:2), the side condition $j\ne p\vee k\ne q\vee|\ell-m|>1$.
\end{itemize}
A relation $a=b$ is encoded as the relator $ab^{-1}$, and the commutator relations as the commutators themselves. For example, (Im:5a) appears as $\tw_{\ell,jk}H_1(s^{j-\ell})\tw_{\ell,jk}^{-1}=H_2\big((s^{\ell+1})^{-1}\big)$, which is the source relation.
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{Rel} --- library, verified earlier}

\paragraph{The group.} \leanname{Grp} $c$ is the presented group $G_c:=F(\mathcal{X})/N_{\mathcal{R}}$. For a word $w\in F(\mathcal{X})$ we write $\cl{w}\in G_c$ for its image under the canonical homomorphism $F(\mathcal{X})\to G_c$. When $c$ is the coefficient function of $J$, $G_c$ is $G(\mathfrak{m})$.
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{Grp} --- library, verified earlier}

\paragraph{Library theorems used.} Two library theorems are used below.
\begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{mk_eqr}: if $\leanname{eqr}(a,b)=ab^{-1}$ is a relator, then $\cl{a}=\cl{b}$ in $G_c$.
\item \leanname{h2_eq_conj}: for every $s\in\Cx$,
\[
\cl{H_2(s)}=\cl{\tw_{-1}}\,\cl{H_1(s)}\,\cl{\tw_{-1}}^{-1}.
\]
This follows from (Re:5a) and \leanname{mk_eqr}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{The target statement}

\begin{leanblock}{Lean: target statement \texttt{gsimple\_torus\_normalizes\_unipotent}, fixed before the proof; \texttt{solution} is checked to have exactly this type}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{gsimple\PYZus{}torus\PYZus{}normalizes\PYZus{}unipotent}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{torusSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{unipSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{unipSub}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

The target statement says the following: for every function $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$, every $h\in\leanname{torusSub}\,c$ and every $u\in\leanname{unipSub}\,c$, we have $huh^{-1}\in\leanname{unipSub}\,c$. This is the theorem of the source, with $c$ standing for the coefficient function of $J$.

The only parameter is $c$, and the statement carries no hypothesis on it. In particular the hypothesis $c(2)\ge1$, which the library needs for Theorem 3.3, does not appear. The source statement is therefore the special case where $c$ is the coefficient function of $J$; the formal statement holds for every $c$. The theorem \leanname{solution} below has exactly this statement.

\section{Proof}

\subsection{Overview}

The source proof has three steps.
\begin{enumerate}
\item By (Re:6a)--(Re:6d) and (Im:6a)--(Im:6d), conjugation by $H_1(s)$ or $H_2(s)$ sends a unipotent generator into $U$.
\item The source writes $h\in H$ as a product of generators and inverses of generators. It then concludes, by induction on the number of factors, that $hgh^{-1}\in U$ for every generator $g$ of $U$.
\item The source writes $u\in U$ as a product of generators of $U$ and their inverses. It then uses $huh^{-1}=\prod_i hg_ih^{-1}$ and $hg^{-1}h^{-1}=(hgh^{-1})^{-1}$.
\end{enumerate}

The formal proof proceeds as follows.
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Step 1.} \Cref{lem:h1_conj_unip,lem:h2_conj_unip,lem:torusGens_conj} prove step 1 in a sharper form: the conjugate of a unipotent generator by $H_1(s)$ or $H_2(s)$ is again a unipotent generator.
\item \textbf{Step 3.} \Cref{lem:conj_closure_of_gens} is step 3, proved for an arbitrary group and an arbitrary generating set.
\item \textbf{Step 2.} \Cref{lem:closure_normalizes} is step 2, also proved abstractly.
\end{itemize}

The order of steps 2 and 3 is exchanged. The formal proof first fixes a single generator $t$ of $H$ and extends ``$t$ conjugates $U$ into $U$'' from the generators of $U$ to all of $U$. Only then does it induct over the word for $h$.

The formal proof deviates from the source on one point. In step 2 the source admits inverses of generators among the factors $h_i$, but then treats each $h_i$ as some $H_1(s_i)$ or $H_2(s_i)$. This tacitly uses that $H_1(s)^{-1}$ and $H_2(s)^{-1}$ are again of this form. The formal proof establishes this explicitly in two lemmas.
\begin{itemize}
\item \Cref{lem:h1_inv_mk} gives $\cl{H_1(s)}^{-1}=\cl{H_1(s^{-1})}$. It is derived from (H:1a).
\item \Cref{lem:h2_inv_mk} gives the same for $H_2$. It is derived from (Re:5a), via the library theorem \leanname{h2_eq_conj}, together with \cref{lem:h1_inv_mk}. Relation (H:1b) would also suffice, but it is not the route taken.
\end{itemize}
\Cref{lem:torusGens_inv} then states that the generating set of $H$ is closed under inverses. This closure is the hypothesis that lets the abstract induction of \cref{lem:closure_normalizes} handle inverse factors.

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{h1_conj_unip}]\label{lem:h1_conj_unip}
Let $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$, $s\in\Cx$, and let $g\in G_c$ belong to \leanname{unipGens} $c$. Then
\[
\cl{H_1(s)}\,g\,\cl{H_1(s)}^{-1}\in\leanname{unipGens}\,c.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
There are four cases, according to the form of $g$. In each case the relevant relator lies in $\mathcal{R}$, so \leanname{mk_eqr} gives an equality in $G_c$. Its left-hand side equals $\cl{H_1(s)}\,g\,\cl{H_1(s)}^{-1}$, because $w\mapsto\cl{w}$ is a homomorphism.
\begin{itemize}
\item If $g=\cl{X_{-1}(u)}$, then (Re:6a) gives $\cl{H_1(s)X_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}}=\cl{X_{-1}(su)}$.
\item If $g=\cl{Y_{-1}(u)}$, then (Re:6c) gives $\cl{Y_{-1}(s^{-1}u)}$.
\item If $g=\cl{X_{\ell,jk}(u)}$ with $(\ell,j,k)\in E$, then (Im:6a) gives $\cl{X_{\ell,jk}(s^{\ell+1}u)}$.
\item If $g=\cl{Y_{\ell,jk}(u)}$ with $(\ell,j,k)\in E$, then (Im:6c) gives $\cl{Y_{\ell,jk}((s^{\ell+1})^{-1}u)}$.
\end{itemize}
In each case the right-hand side is the image of a unipotent generator, with the same index $(\ell,j,k)\in E$ in the last two cases. It therefore lies in \leanname{unipGens} $c$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{h2_conj_unip}]\label{lem:h2_conj_unip}
Let $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$, $s\in\Cx$, and let $g\in G_c$ belong to \leanname{unipGens} $c$. Then
\[
\cl{H_2(s)}\,g\,\cl{H_2(s)}^{-1}\in\leanname{unipGens}\,c.
\]
\end{lemma}

\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
The argument is the same as for \cref{lem:h1_conj_unip}, using (Re:6b), (Re:6d), (Im:6b), (Im:6d) with \leanname{mk_eqr}. The conjugate equals, respectively,
\[
\cl{X_{-1}(s^{-1}u)},\qquad \cl{Y_{-1}(su)},\qquad \cl{X_{\ell,jk}(s^{j-\ell}u)},\qquad \cl{Y_{\ell,jk}((s^{j-\ell})^{-1}u)}.
\]
Each of these is the image of a unipotent generator.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{h1_inv_mk}]\label{lem:h1_inv_mk}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1: $\cl{H_1(1)}=1$.} Relation (H:1a) with $s=t=1$ gives $\cl{H_1(1)}\,\cl{H_1(1)}=\cl{H_1(1)}$. Cancelling one factor yields $\cl{H_1(1)}=1$.

\emph{Step 2.} Relation (H:1a) with $t=s^{-1}$, together with $ss^{-1}=1$ in $\Cx$ and \leanname{mk_eqr}, gives $\cl{H_1(s)}\,\cl{H_1(s^{-1})}=\cl{H_1(1)}=1$. Multiplying on the left by $\cl{H_1(s)}^{-1}$ gives the claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{h2_inv_mk}]\label{lem:h2_inv_mk}
For every $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ and every $s\in\Cx$, we have $\cl{H_2(s^{-1})}=\cl{H_2(s)}^{-1}$ in $G_c$.
\end{lemma}

\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
Write $w=\cl{\tw_{-1}}$. By the library theorem \leanname{h2_eq_conj}, which follows from (Re:5a), applied to $s^{-1}$ and to $s$, and by \cref{lem:h1_inv_mk},
\[
\cl{H_2(s^{-1})}=w\,\cl{H_1(s^{-1})}\,w^{-1}=w\,\cl{H_1(s)}^{-1}w^{-1}=\big(w\,\cl{H_1(s)}\,w^{-1}\big)^{-1}=\cl{H_2(s)}^{-1}.\qedhere
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{torusGens_conj}]\label{lem:torusGens_conj}
Let $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$. For every $t\in\leanname{torusGens}\,c$ and every $g\in\leanname{unipGens}\,c$, we have $tgt^{-1}\in\leanname{unipGens}\,c$.
\end{lemma}

By definition $t=\cl{H_1(s)}$ or $t=\cl{H_2(s)}$ for some $s\in\Cx$. The claim is then \cref{lem:h1_conj_unip} or \cref{lem:h2_conj_unip} respectively.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{torusGens_inv}]\label{lem:torusGens_inv}
Let $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$. If $t\in\leanname{torusGens}\,c$, then $t^{-1}\in\leanname{torusGens}\,c$.
\end{lemma}

\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
If $t=\cl{H_1(s)}$, then $t^{-1}=\cl{H_1(s^{-1})}$ by \cref{lem:h1_inv_mk}. If $t=\cl{H_2(s)}$, then $t^{-1}=\cl{H_2(s^{-1})}$ by \cref{lem:h2_inv_mk}. In both cases $s^{-1}\in\Cx$, so $t^{-1}\in\leanname{torusGens}\,c$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{conj_closure_of_gens}]\label{lem:conj_closure_of_gens}
Let $G$ be a group, $T\subseteq G$ a subset and $x\in G$. Suppose $xgx^{-1}\in\langle T\rangle$ for every $g\in T$. Then $xux^{-1}\in\langle T\rangle$ for every $u\in\langle T\rangle$.
\end{lemma}

\PY{+w}{      }\PY{k}{have}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hxa}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{o}{)}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⁻¹}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{group}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{hxa}\PY{o}{]}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Subgroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inv\PYZus{}mem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{iha}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
We use induction over the generation of $\langle T\rangle$. Every element of $\langle T\rangle$ is obtained from elements of $T$ and from $1$ by products and inverses, so it suffices to check that the property ``$xux^{-1}\in\langle T\rangle$'' is preserved by these operations.
\begin{itemize}
\item For $u\in T$ it holds by hypothesis.
\item For $u=1$ we have $x1x^{-1}=1\in\langle T\rangle$.
\item If it holds for $a$ and $b$, then $x(ab)x^{-1}=(xax^{-1})(xbx^{-1})\in\langle T\rangle$.
\item If it holds for $a$, then $xa^{-1}x^{-1}=(xax^{-1})^{-1}\in\langle T\rangle$.\qedhere
\end{itemize}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{closure_normalizes}]\label{lem:closure_normalizes}
Let $G$ be a group and $S,T\subseteq G$. Assume:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $t^{-1}\in S$ for every $t\in S$;
\item $tut^{-1}\in\langle T\rangle$ for every $t\in S$ and every $u\in\langle T\rangle$.
\end{enumerate}
Then $huh^{-1}\in\langle T\rangle$ for every $h\in\langle S\rangle$ and every $u\in\langle T\rangle$.
\end{lemma}

\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
Let $P(h)$ be the statement ``$huh^{-1}\in\langle T\rangle$ for all $u\in\langle T\rangle$''. Every element of $\langle S\rangle$ is obtained from $1$ by successive left multiplications by elements $x\in S$ or by inverses $x^{-1}$ of elements $x\in S$. It therefore suffices to prove three things.
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{$P(1)$ holds}, since $1u1^{-1}=u$.
\item \emph{If $x\in S$, $y\in\langle S\rangle$ and $P(y)$, then $P(xy)$.} Indeed
\[
(xy)u(xy)^{-1}=x\,(yuy^{-1})\,x^{-1}.
\]
Here $yuy^{-1}\in\langle T\rangle$ by $P(y)$, so hypothesis~(2) applied to $x$ gives the claim.
\item \emph{If $x\in S$, $y\in\langle S\rangle$ and $P(y)$, then $P(x^{-1}y)$.} Indeed
\[
(x^{-1}y)u(x^{-1}y)^{-1}=x^{-1}\,(yuy^{-1})\,(x^{-1})^{-1}.
\]
By hypothesis~(1), $x^{-1}\in S$, so hypothesis~(2) applied to $x^{-1}$ and to $yuy^{-1}\in\langle T\rangle$ gives the claim.\qedhere
\end{itemize}
\end{proof}

\PY{+w}{      }\PY{o}{(}\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{g}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hg}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=\PYZgt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Subgroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{subset\PYZus{}closure}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{torusGens\PYZus{}conj}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{t}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ht}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{g}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hg}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hh}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{u}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hu}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

\begin{proof}
Fix $c\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$. Apply \cref{lem:closure_normalizes} in the group $G_c$ with
\[
S=\leanname{torusGens}\,c,\qquad T=\leanname{unipGens}\,c.
\]
Then $\langle S\rangle=\leanname{torusSub}\,c$ and $\langle T\rangle=\leanname{unipSub}\,c$. The two hypotheses are checked as follows.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The closure of $S$ under inverses is \cref{lem:torusGens_inv}.
\item Let $t\in S$. For every $g\in T$, \cref{lem:torusGens_conj} gives $tgt^{-1}\in T\subseteq\langle T\rangle$. So \cref{lem:conj_closure_of_gens}, applied with $x=t$, gives $tut^{-1}\in\langle T\rangle$ for every $u\in\langle T\rangle$.
\end{enumerate}
\Cref{lem:closure_normalizes} then yields $huh^{-1}\in\leanname{unipSub}\,c$ for all $h\in\leanname{torusSub}\,c$ and all $u\in\leanname{unipSub}\,c$. This is the target statement.
\end{proof}

\section{Independent review}

A referee model (claude-opus-5-5), which took no part in the formalization or the proof, compared the verified Lean file with the source. Verdict: \textbf{FAITHFUL}.

\paragraph{Definitions.} I checked each object in the \leanname{GM} namespace against the source.

\begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{Valid} encodes the index set $E$: it requires $\ell<j$ and $1\le k\le c(j)$. Since $\ell\ge 0$, this forces $j\ge 1$. The function $c:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ is a parameter, as the formalization note allows.
\item \leanname{Gen} has one constructor for each family of symbols in $\mathcal{X}$. These are $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$ for $s\in\mathbb{C}^\times$; $X_{-1}(u)$ and $Y_{-1}(u)$ for $u\in\mathbb{C}$; and $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ and $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$, which carry a proof of \leanname{Valid}. That proof is a Prop, so it adds no spurious distinct generators.
\item \leanname{cc} is $(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell)$. The truncated subtraction $j-\ell$ is harmless because $\ell<j$.
\item \leanname{wm} matches (Re:0) and \leanname{ww} matches (Im:0).
\item \leanname{cm} is $aba^{-1}b^{-1}$ and \leanname{eqr} is $ab^{-1}$, as in the source.
\item \leanname{Rel} has one constructor for every tagged relation, from (H:1a) through (U:4b). I compared each one with the source, including:
  \begin{itemize}
  \item the scalars in (Re:2), (Im:2), (Re:3) and (Im:3);
  \item the inverted exponents in (Im:5a), (Im:5b), (Im:6c) and (Im:6d);
  \item the index $j-1$ in (U:1a) and (U:1b);
  \item the condition $|\ell-m|>1$ in (U:2), written with \leanname{Int.natAbs};
  \item the sign $(-1)^{j-\ell-1}$ in (U:4a) and (U:4b).
  \end{itemize}
  All agree. In (U:4a) and (U:4b) the extra hypothesis that $(j-1-\ell,j,k)$ is valid follows automatically from validity of $(\ell,j,k)$, so it does not weaken the relations.
\item \leanname{Grp} is the presented group $F(\mathcal{X})/N_{\mathcal{R}}$.
\item \leanname{torusSub} is the subgroup generated by the images of all $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$; this is $H$.
\item \leanname{unipSub} is the subgroup generated by the images of all four families of unipotent generators; this is $U$.
\end{itemize}

I found no discrepancies.

\paragraph{Statement.} The target says: for every $c:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$, every $h\in$ \leanname{torusSub} $c$ and every $u\in$ \leanname{unipSub} $c$, the element $huh^{-1}$ lies in \leanname{unipSub} $c$. This is exactly the source conclusion.

No hypothesis on $c$ is needed. To recover the source statement, instantiate $c(j)$ as the coefficient of $q^j$ in $J$ for $j\ge 1$. These coefficients are nonnegative integers, and $c(0)=0$ is consistent with the source. Only $j\ge 1$ is ever used.

The result is not vacuous. The relations are those of the source, so the group is not degenerate by construction, and $H$ and $U$ are the genuine subgroups described there.

\paragraph{Proof.} The lemmas from \leanname{mk_rel_one} through \leanname{gsimple_g_m_perfect} are carried over from the Theorem 3.3 development. \leanname{solution} does not use them.

The relevant part of the proof runs as follows.
\begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{h1_conj_unip} and \leanname{h2_conj_unip} show that conjugating a unipotent generator by $H_1(s)$ or $H_2(s)$ gives another unipotent generator. They use (Re:6a) to (Re:6d) and (Im:6a) to (Im:6d). This is the first paragraph of the source proof.
\item \leanname{torusGens_inv} shows that the generating set of $H$ is closed under inverses. It relies on two auxiliary lemmas:
  \begin{itemize}
  \item \leanname{h1_inv_mk} proves $H_1(s^{-1})=H_1(s)^{-1}$ from (H:1a);
  \item \leanname{h2_inv_mk} proves $H_2(s^{-1})=H_2(s)^{-1}$ by writing $H_2$ as a conjugate of $H_1$ via (Re:5a).
  \end{itemize}
  The source silently allows "generators or their inverses". The formal proof justifies inverses explicitly, using (Re:5a) rather than the analogous (H:1b) for $H_2$. This is a legitimate deviation.
\item \leanname{conj_closure_of_gens} carries out the source's last paragraph. By closure induction, conjugation by a fixed $x$ that maps generators into $U$ maps all of $U$ into $U$; inverses of generators are handled as in the source.
\item \leanname{closure_normalizes} carries out the source's induction over a word $h=h_1\cdots h_m$, using \leanname{Subgroup.closure_induction_left}.
\end{itemize}

All of these lemmas state exactly the step they represent.

\paragraph{Concerns.} \begin{itemize}
\item The coefficients $c(j)$ of $J$ are a free parameter. The theorem is proved for every $c$, so it covers the actual coefficients.
\item The file contains a large block of unused lemmas from the Theorem 3.3 development. They are harmless to this result.
\end{itemize}
